FAREWELL TO THE RETIRING MEMBERS 
MR. CHAIRMAN: Hon'ble Members, today, we bid farewell to some of our esteemed 
colleagues who would be retiring in the months of April to July this year on completion 
of their term of office. A total of 59 Member s from 20 States will be retiring during this 
period, including 9 women Members. 
Hon'ble Members, our constitutional scheme provides for the retirement of 
one-third of the Members of this House ev ery two years, thereby bringing about a 
periodic change in the composition of this august House. This arrangement ensures 
continuity in the functioning of the Council of States while also creating space for new 
Members to join the House and enrich it s deliberations with their experience and 
insights. This process truly reflects the character of this House as a microcosm of our 
diverse nation. 
26
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
Hon'ble Members, retirement should no t be viewed as an  end, but as the 
beginning of new roles and resp onsibilities. It signifies the passing on of the baton to 
new Members who will carry forward the legacy  of this institution with fresh ideas, 
renewed energy and commitment. At the same time, the experience and wisdom 
gained by the retiring Members during their tenure will continue to guide and inspire 
the next generation of pa rliamentarians. Among the Me mbers retiring are several 
distinguished public figures and veteran par liamentarians who have served the nation 
with dedication for many years in public life. Shriman H. D. Devegowda, former Prime 
Minister of India, is a distinguished leader whose presence has added great stature to 
the House.  With his long experience in pa rliamentary and public life and his deep 
commitment to the interests of farmers and rural communities, he has brought 
valuable perspectives to the debates of th is House.  The House has also benefited 
greatly from the leadership and experience of Shriman Mallikarjun Kharge, the hon. 
Leader of the Opposition in the Rajya Sabha.  With his long and distinguished 
parliamentary career in both the Lok Sa bha and the Rajya Sabha, Shriman Kharge 
has been an important voice in the democratic functioning of this House.  I would also 
like to make a particular mention of Shrima n Harivansh, the hon. Deputy Chairman of 
the Rajya Sabha, whose term is also coming to an end on 9 th April 2026.  Shriman 
Harivansh has presided over this House with  dignity, impartiality and a deep sense of 
responsibility. Before entering parliamentar y life, he had a distinguished career in 
journalism and public discourse, and he br ought to this House his vast experience, 
calm demeanour and commitment to parliame ntary traditions. As Deputy Chairman, 
he has guided the proceedings of the Ho use with patience, fairness and wisdom, 
earning the respect and appreciation of Members across the political spectrum.  
Furthermore, he has made meaningful and va luable contributions in several national 
and international parliamentary forums, including the Inter-Parliamentary Union 
(IPU), the Commonwealth Parliament ary Association (CPA), the BRICS 
Parliamentary Forum, the CSPOC and th e P-20 Parliamentary Speakers’ Summit.  
His contribution towards maintaining the decorum and orderly functioning of the 
House will always be remembered with great regard.  I also record my appreciation to 
retiring Members of the Panel of the Vice -Chairpersons whose contributions as the 
Presiding Officer have enabled the smoot h conduct of Business of the House.  
Among the retiring Members are also seve ral distinguished personalities who have 
made notable contributions in governance  and public life.  The House has also 
benefited from the experience of those Members who have served the country in 
executive and administrative capacities.  All the retiring Members have been articulate 
voices representing diverse regions and communities of our country. Most of them 
[ 18 March, 2026 ]
27

 
have long experience in legislative affair s and have contributed significantly to the 
debates of the House.  They have repres ented the aspirations of their respective 
States and communities and the nation as a whole and have raised important issues 
related thereto in this House.  Hon. Me mbers, a beautiful sentiment expressed in 
Tamil reminds us: & “The benefits of good deeds done  by a person, will live forever 
transcending time”, which conveys that though one retires, the good done by one’s 
deeds continues to live on through time.   
The retiring Members of this House have, through their public service and 
dedication, created such enduring contributions that will remain part of the legacy of 
this institution.  Their insights and in terventions have enriched the debates and 
discussions of this House and streng thened the democratic process. The 
contributions made by the women Member s who are retiring are particularly 
noteworthy and reflect their steadfast commitment to public service.   
I would like to place on record my si ncere appreciation for the valuable 
contributions made by the retiring Members and for the cooperation and support they 
have extended to the Chair in the conduct of the proceed ings of the House.  Some 
among the retiring Members have already been  re-elected to this House. I am sure a 
few more will be re-elected and their experience will once again enrich the 
deliberations of this august institution. 
The event of farewell is always a painful mo ment marked by a sense of parting, yet 
made meaningful by the lasting memories and contributions that endure.  
As they conclude their term in this august House, I wish all the retiring 
members good health, happiness and succe ss in their future endeavors. I am 
confident that they will continue to serve the nation and the people with the same 
dedication and commitment that they have de monstrated during their tenure in this 
House. Now, hon'ble Prime Minister.

SHRI SUBHAS CHANDRA BOSE PILLI (Andhra Pradesh): Hon. Chairman Sir, please 
permit me to speak in Telugu language.  & “ Sir, I convey my heartfelt greetings to 
you, to all the leaders present in this Hous e, and also to my friends and seniors who 
are due to retire shortly.   Chairman Sir, today is also a time and occasion for me to 
pay tribute to two great personalities who infl uenced my political life. One is the late 
Sri Rayavaram Munasabu Garu, who is a veteran Congress leader and a very close 
associate of the former Chief Minister Ka su Brahmananda Reddy Garu. The other is 
the former Chief Minister of Andhra Prad esh, our beloved leader Dr. Rajasekhar 
Reddy Garu. Remembering both of them, I would like to express my heartfelt 
gratitude on this occasion. 
Sir, while one generally ought not to ma ke references to caste within this 
House, I belong to the Setti Balija community, which is categorized under the OBC 
category. In the 75 years since independence, I stand here as the ve ry first individual 
from my community to step into this Pa rliament. It is through the kind grace and 
immense support of our leader, Sri Ja gan Mohan Reddy ga ru and with the 
encouragement he provided that I became the first person from my community to 
ascend the steps of the Parliament. I wish  to convey my special and profound 
gratitude to him. 
Sir, this institution is a sacred temple of democracy. Yet, we find ourselves in a 
distressing predicament where we are rend ered helpless and unable to intervene, as 
                   
& English translation of the original speech delivered in Telugu. 
44
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
the very laws enacted within  this temple are being dismantled and riddled with 
loopholes right before our eyes. Therefore,  I have utilized the opportunity you have 
granted me today to bring this critical matter to the attention of the Government, 
through you, Sir. 
First is the Representation of the People Act. In this Act, disqualification of 
members, on whom is it placed? The judgment  has to be given by the Speaker of the 
Legislative Assembly or the Parliament Speak e r  o r  i t  l i e s  w i t h  y o u .  I f  w e  b r i n g  a  
politician and make him sit as a judge in a court, what justice will happen, Chairman?  
The Representation of the People Act was enacted with a sacred  vision and a noble 
objective. Yet, right before ou r very eyes, we witness acts going  against this very law 
and instances where individuals resign midway  through their term from the party that 
elected them, only to win an election repr esenting a different party.  In such 
situations, we find ourselves powerless to intervene. Under these circumstances, 
how can respect for these legislative bodies grow? How can public faith in our 
democracy be strengthened? Why would the people place their trust in us? 
Consequently, this breeds a deep sense of mistrust among the public. It creates a 
situation where people begin to question th e very utility of the laws enacted by the 
Parliament.  I earnestly appeal to the hon. Prime Minister to give this matter serious 
consideration that if any individual defects from their political party, they must be 
disqualified from contesting elections for the remainder of that specific term. Only 
then will the noble objectiv e behind the enactment of th is law be truly fulfilled.  
Furthermore, it is imperative to urgently remove the Speaker from the position of 
adjudicator and entrust this judicial function to the High Court. In my view, this is the 
only way to ensure that justice is truly se rved. Moreover, a strict time limit, perhaps 
three to six months, should be fixed for the adjudication of such cases. If such 
measures are implemented, the law will ef fectively serve the sacred purpose and 
noble objective for which it was orig inally enacted, thereby restoring and 
strengthening the public's faith in our democracy.  
Similarly, the second law is the Info rmation Act, specifically, the Right to 
Information Act. This is a law enacted directly by Parliament, a statute introduced by 
the hon. Prime Minister, Dr. Manmohan Singh, during his tenure. Yet, despite the 
existence of this Act, the manner in which administration is being conducted in the 
States is such that numerous Government  Orders (GOs) are being concealed from 
the public. Then, what is th e purpose of that law? State Legislative Assemblies are 
rejecting a law enacted by the Parliament. If  we merely stand by and watch this, it 
implies that wherever there is secrecy, there is bound to be either corruption, 
[ 18 March, 2026 ]
45

 
nepotism, or a motive to protect one's own faction and there is, certainly, something 
untoward.  Sir, if we, too, remain silent spectators and thereby become complicit in 
this, then, what is the point of having a Parliament here? Why should Parliament 
enact laws at all? If those laws hold no va lue, how can the public's faith in the system 
ever grow? I earnestly urge the Government  to reflect upon all these matters. Sir, 
there are many such instances . I am bringing only two such laws to your attention 
today. Where will justice be served? Where will respect for the laws we enact ever 
increase? Where will public trust ever grow ? Tell me. At the very least, the power to 
rectify this lies in our hands and there is an urgent need to amend this legislation. If we 
are unable to do so, it would be better to simply repeal these laws altogether.   
When laws exist, yet despite their existence, we find ourselves in a helpless 
situation where we can do nothing but watch with our eyes wide open, then what is 
the purpose of the Prime Minister's office ? What is the purpose of the Speaker's 
office? What is the purpose of the Vice President's office? All these positions become 
futile. Sir, if we are reduced to such a depl orable state in this country, where we are 
unable to even implement the very laws we ourselves have enacted, then the entire 
exercise becomes utterly pointless. Please forgive me for my candour, Chairman Sir, 
but in the short time I have been here, I have observed that when our own leaders fail 
to show respect for the laws we have create d, then, what is the point of having these 
laws? What utility do they serve? It is absolu tely imperative that this trend be curbed. 
If we wish to foster greater faith in our de mocracy, it is absolutely essential that we 
put a stop to this immediately.  
Sir, I make this earnest appeal to you on this occasion. Indeed, there are many 
such instances that could be cited. Please give this some thought. I have spoken to 
my leader Mr. Jagan Mohan Re ddy many times. Sir, I have  mentioned this to many 
people among my friends on several occasio ns. There are two towering personalities 
present in this august House, one is Dr. Manmohan Singh garu  and the other is       
Shri Deve Gowda garu. Both are former Prime Ministers, and I have been granted the 
privilege of sitting alongside them by my leader Shri Jagan Mohan Reddy garu.  I have 
stated on numerous occasions to my frie nds that there are many other eminent 
personalities present here as well. Sir, the very laws we have enacted are being 
dismantled right before our eyes. We find ourselves in a helpless situation where we 
can do nothing but watch as these laws are torn to shreds and destroyed right in front 
of us.  Sir, is this the reas on we sit in this Parliament? Is this the reason we occupy 
these high offices? Is this the reason the people elected and sent us here? With what 
aspirations did we enact these laws? What were the specific goals we intended to

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
achieve?   If those very goals are being th warted and prevented from being realized 
then this entire exercise becomes futile. It serves no purpose for democracy. Indeed, 
democracy itself faces the threat of extinction. If, knowingly or unknowingly, we 
encourage such trends, democracy will inevit ably perish. Therefor e, Chairman Sir,

sincerity, devotion and dedication with  what they did. Shri Muzibulla Khan , right 
across his entire term in this House had a deep concern for the people not only of 
Odisha but across India. He spoke for the under-privileged; he spoke for the 
minorities; and, he spoke for the poor.  When he rose to speak, one could sense he 
was not speaking merely as a parliamentaria n, he was speaking as a representative 
who carried the anxieties, aspirations, and quiet hopes of the people who have sent 
him here.  Shri Niranjan Bishi j brought to this Chamber a voice that was calm but 
deeply purposeful. He spoke for the marginalised, for the tribal  communities, for the 
weaker sections of society that have long waited for resonance. He did so not only 
with dignity, without anger, without spectacle, but with conviction.  That is the kind of 
service that does not go unnoticed.

to that.  Sir, we will miss you, but I am very hopeful you will come back.

DR. M. THAMBIDURAI (Tamil Nadu) :  Hon. Chairman Sir. Tod ay, I stand before this 
august House for what is likely my address after fulfilling the present term of six years 
as a Member of Rajya Sabha from Tamil Na du. Representing All India Anna Dravida 
Munnetra Kazhagam, I am filled with a d eep sense of gratitude, humility and 
reflection.  Though I have b een re-elected, I will continue to serve the country as a 
Member of this august House. I am duty-b ound to deliver this farewell speech with a 
heavy heart. This House has been my second home, a place where I have witn essed 
the finest traditions of parliamentary democracy, debated issues of national 
importance and contributed to the welfare of  our people, particularly those of Tamil 
Nadu.  I began my journey in public se rvice inspired by the timeless ideals of Thanthai 
52
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
Periyar, Perarignar Anna, and Puratchi Thalaivar  MGR and later guided by the 
steadfast leadership of Amma, Puratchi Thalaivi Jayalalithaa. The Dravidian principles 
that form the very foundation of our mo vement - self-respect, social justice, 
rationalism and federal equality - have been my constant compass.  These principles 
teach us that every individual deserves di gnity without discrimination; that society 
must be rebuilt on equality, eradicatin g caste and empowering the backward and 
marginalised through affirmative action and inclusive welfare; that blind faith and 
superstition must give way to scientific te mper, critical thinking and moral education; 
that true federalism ensures States like Ta mil Nadu receive equal resources; that we 
must respect our language and culture and autonomy to pursue developments suited 
to our needs. 
I have always insisted in the House to make all the languages of the country as 
official national languages.  This is the most important thing, which I have always 
raised in the House.  This is especially the Upper House.  This is called the Council of 
States.  This is the place where we can raise our voice for rights of equality 
throughout this country.  These are not mere slogans; they are a living philosophy that 
has transformed us. 
Today, I express my profound thanks to  the leaders of my beloved AIADMK, 
respected General-Secretary of our Party an d the former Chief Minister, who is going 
to be the next Chief Minister soon, Shri  Edappadi K. Palaniswamy, and cadets who

domination; to oppose the imposition of Hindi; to reject vi olence and eradicate

States. These ideals form the foundation of my public life, and I shall continue to 
uphold them with commitment and conviction.  Before I conclude, I wish to share two 
important reflections. 
First, Dr. Kalaignar once said that in a parliamentary democracy, the majority is 
like the body, while the minority is like the ey es. The body must act, but it is the eyes 
that provide vision. Therefore, the majority must remain sensitive to the concerns and 
perspectives of the minority. However, it is my respectful submission that this spirit 
has weakened in recent years. I urge th e Government to uphold this democratic 
principle by giving due respect and consid eration to the voices of the minority.  
Second, Perarignar Anna had once observed that “the North flourishes while the 
South perishes. ” Decades have passed, yet the im balance he referred to has not 
been fully addressed. In fact, there is a gr owing perception that regional disparities 
continue to persist. Today we stand at a point where it feels like “the North lives and it 
makes the South suffer.”  This must change. India’s progress must be inclusive, 
balanced, and equitable across all region s—economically, socially, and politically. 
Only then can we truly realise the vision of a united and prosperous nation. With these 
words, I conclude my speech. I once ag ain thank you for this opportunity. 
Vanakkam.”

SHRI NIRANJAN BISHI (Odisha) : Hon. Chairman, Sir, and respected Members of 
this august House, today, when I rise to sp eak in this dignified Chamber, my heart is 
filled with deep gratitude and a profound se nse of responsibility. The Parliament of 
India is not merely a building of debate, discussion or deliberation, it is the sacred 
temple of our democracy. Ours is the larges t democratic country in the world. It is 
here that the dreams of We, the people of India, as enshrined in the Preamble of our 
Constitution, find voice, direction and action.  Being entrusted to serve in this august 
House has been one of the greatest honour s of my life. Our Constitution, drafted 
[ 18 March, 2026 ]
71

 
under the visionary leadership of Dr. B. R. Ambedkar, is not merely a legal document, 
but a moral commitment to justice, liberty, equality and fraternity. Throughout my 
tenure, I have sincerely tried to uphold these constitution al values, while raising the 
voice of the marginalised sections of th e society, particularly Scheduled Castes, 
Scheduled Tribes, farmers, labourers, workers, toiling masses, women and the other 
marginalised communities of this society. 
  Sir, coming from the tribal heartland of Odisha, I have always felt that my 
presence in this House carried the aspirati ons and hopes of millions who historically 
remained unheard.  Whenever  I spoke, I did not speak merely as an individual 
Member, I spoke as a representative of the village, forest and communities whose 
faith in the democracy is deep  and unwavering.  During my tenure, I tried to highlight 
several important issues like protection of trib al rights, preservation of tribal language 
and culture, development of backward regi on, strengthening of education, health, 
agriculture, welfare schemes for SC, ST and OBC communities and safeguarding the 
constitutional rights of the Scheduled Castes and the Scheduled Tribes.  These 
issues are not merely policy concerns.  They represent the dignity, identity and future 
of millions of citizens.  India's democracy is strong because of its institution, its 
diversity, and its spirit of dialogue.  The Rajya Sabha, as the Council of States, the 
House of Lords, or the House of Elders, plays a crucial role in the strengthening of our 
federal structure and ensuring that every re gion of our country is heard in national 
policy making.  
At this moment, I offer my heartfelt thanks and sincere gratitude to my beloved 
leader, the President of BJD and the former  Chief Minister of Odisha, Shri Naveen 
Patnaik, whose visionary leadership , commitment to clean governance and 
dedication to the welfare of the people of  Odisha have inspired countless public 
servants like me.   
I also extend my sincere thanks to you, our hon. Chairman, and Shri 
Harivansh, hon. Deputy Chairman, the Pane l of Vice-Chairmen, hon. Leader of the 
House, hon. Leader of the Opposition, Shri  Mallikarjun Kharge, all the hon. Ministers 
and all the leaders of political parties, my  floor leader, Dr. Sasmit Patra, and my 
respected fellow Members of Parliament across all party lines.  I thank the Secretary-
General, Shri P.C. Mody, and all the dedi cated officers and staff of the Rajya Sabha 
Secretariat who tirelessly uphold the dignity and functioning of this great institution - 
Rajya Sabha.

Public life does not end with the comple tion of a parliamentary term; service to 
society is a lifelong duty.  Even after leav ing this House, I shall remain committed to 
the ideals of our Constitution, to the empowerment of Scheduled Caste communities, 
Scheduled Tribe communities, the toiling mass and the marginalised communities of 
India.  Let us remember that the true strength  of India lies in its villages, its youth, its 
farmers, its workers, and its tribal comm unities.  Our responsibility, as public 
representative, is to ensure that developm ent, justice and opportunity reach the last 
person in this line, as envisioned by the ma ker of our Constitution, Baba Saheb

Mata.  Thank you, Sir.  Jai Hind!

دﻧــوں  اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘــــﯽ  رﮨــﺎ ﮨـــوں،۔  ﻣﯾں  ،ﻧوﻣــﺑﺮ2020 ﻣﯾں  رﭨـــﺎﺋﺮ  ﮨــوا ﺗﮫـــﺎ  اور  ،ﺟوﻻﺋــﯽ 2022 ﺗک 
اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﺗﮫــــﺎ۔  ﺑﮩت ﺳﺎرے اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  اور  اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﺑﮩت وﯾوﺳﺘﮫت ﺟﯾون ﺟﯾﺘﮯ ﮨﯾـــں۔  
اﻧﮩﯾـــں  ﮐﺳﯽ ﺳﭘورٹ ﯾــﺎ ﮐﺳﯽ ﻣــدد  ﮐﯽ ﺿﺮورت ﻧﮩﯾــں  ﮨوﺗــﯽ  ﮨﮯ۔ اﻧﮐﮯ ﺳﺎرے اﻧﺘظــــﺎم  ﺳدن ﻣﯾں  آﻧﮯ ﺳﮯ  
ﭘﮩﻠﮯ ﺑﮫﯽ ﭘﺮﻓﯾﮐــٹ  ﮨوﺗﮯ ﮨﯾــں  اور  ﺳدن ﺳﮯ  ﺟﺎﻧﮯ ﮐﮯ ﺑﻌــد  ﺑﮫﯽ ﭘﺮﻓﯾﮐــٹ  ﮨوﺗﮯ ﮨﯾـــں۔  ﻟﯾﮐــن ﮐﭼﮫ ﮨﻣـــﺎرے  
ﺟﯾﺳﮯ ﺑﮫﯽ ﮨوﺗﮯ ،ﮨﯾــں  ﺟﻧﮩوں ﻧﮯ ﺟﯾون ﻣﯾں  ﮐﺑﮫﯽ ﮐوﺋﯽ دوﺳﺮا ﮐــﺎم ﻧﮩﯾــں  ﮐﯾـــﺎ  اور  ﺷدھ روپ ﺳﮯ  
راﺟﻧﯾﺘـــک  ﮐﺎرﯾــہ  ﮐﺮﺗہ ﮐﮯ روپ ﻣﯾں  ﺟﯾون وﯾﺘﯾــت  ﮐﺮﻧﮯ ﮐــﺎ  ﻓﯾﺻﻠہ ﮐﯾــــﺎ۔  اﻧﮩﯾـــں  آﻓـــﭨﺮ  رﭨﺎﺋﺮﻣﯾﻧـــٹ 
ﮐﮯ ﺟو ﺑﯾﻧﻔﭨـــس  ﮨوﺗﮯ ،ﯾںه  وه  ﺳﭼﺎرو روپ ﺳﮯ  ﻣﻠﯾــں —اس  ﺑــﺎت ﮐﯽ ﺑﮩت ﺿﺮورت ﮨوﺗــﯽ  ﮨﮯ۔ ﻣﯾں  ﻧﮯ 
ﭘﭼﮫﻠﯽ  ﺑــﺎر ﮐﮩﺎ  ﺗﮫـــﺎ  ﮐہ ﺳﯽ ﺟﯽ اﯾــﭻ  اﯾس ﮐــﺎ  ﻋﻼج اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﮐو آﺟﯾــون  ﻣﻠﺘــــﺎ  ،ﮨﮯ ﻟﯾﮐــن اﺳﮐﯽ ﺑﻠﻧـــگ  
وﻏﯾﺮه  ﮐــﺎ  ﺑﮩت ﻣﺳﺋﻠہ  ﮨوﺗـــﺎ ،ﮨﮯ ﮐﺋﯽ دﻓــﺘﺮوں ﻣﯾں  ﭼﮐﺮ ﻟﮔــﺎﻧﮯ  ﭘﮍﺗﮯ ﮨﯾـــں۔  اس وﻗت ﻣــﺎن  ﺳﮐﮫ 
ﻣﺎﻧـــــڈوﯾﺎ  ﺟﯽ ﺳواﺳﺘﮫہ ﻣﻧــﺘﺮی ﺗﮫﮯ۔ ﮨوںان  ﻧﮯ ﻓﻮﺭﺍً  ﮨﯽ اﺳــﮐﺎ  ﺳـــﻧﮔﯾﺎن  ﻟﯾـــﺎ اور  رﭨـــﺎﺋﺮڈ اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  
ﮐﮯ ﻟﯾﮯ اﯾــک  اﻟــگ  ﺳﮯ  ﮐـــﺎؤﻧﭨﺮ ﮐﮫول ،دﯾـــﺎ  ﻟﯾﮐــن آدھـــﺎ  ﮐــﺎم ﺑــﺎﻗﯽ ره ﮔﯾــــﺎ۔  ﮨﺎﺳـــﭘﭨﻠس  ﻣﯾں  ﺟو 
ﭘﺮوﭨوﮐـــﺎل ڈﭘﺎرﭨﻣﯾﻧــــٹ ،ﮨﮯ ﺟو ﻣوﺟوده  اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﮐــﺎ  ﺑﮍی ﺗﺘﭘﺮﺗـــــﺎ  ﮐﮯ ﺳــﺎﺗﮫ  ﻋﻼج ﮐﺮواﺗـــﺎ ،ﮨﮯ وه  
اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﮐﮯ ﺳــﺎﺗﮫ  وﯾﺳــﺎ  ﻧﮩﯾــں  ﮐﺮﺗـــﺎ۔ ﻣﯾں  اﻧﯾس  ﻣﮩﯾﻧﮯ اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  رﮨــﺎ ،ﮨوں  اﺳﻠﺋﮯ 
ﺟﺎﻧﺘــــﺎ  ﮨوں ﮐہ اﻧﮐـــﺎ رخ ﺑــﮍا ﺑﮯ رﺧﺎ ﮨوﺗـــﺎ ﮨﮯ۔ ﻣﯾں  ﯾہ ﭼﺎﮨوﻧﮔــــﺎ  ﮐہ ﮐوﺋﯽ اﯾﺳﯽ ،اﻧﺳﭨﺮﮐﺷن  
ﻣﺎﻧﻧﯾــــہ  ﺳواﺳﺘﮫ ﻣﻧــﺘﺮی ﺟﯽ ﮐﯽ طﺮف ،ﺳﮯ  ﺟو ﮨﻣـــﺎرے  ﻧﯾﺘــــﺎ ﺳدن ﺑﮫﯽ ،ﮨﯾــں  وه  آج اس وﻗت ﯾﮩــﺎں  ﻧﮩﯾــں

،ﮨﯾــں  اﻧﮐــﯽ  طﺮف ﺳﮯ  ﺗﻣـــﺎم  ﮨﺎﺳـــﭘﭨﻠس  ﮐو ﻧﺮدﯾش ﺟﺎﻧـــﺎ  ﭼــﺎﮨﯾﺋﮯ  ﮐہ ﺳﯽ ﺟﯽ اﯾــﭻ  اﯾس ،ﺑﯾﻧﻔﺷــــﯾﺮی  ﺟو 
اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ،ﮨﯾــں  اﻧﮩﯾـــں  ﺑﮫﯽ ﮐم  ﺳﮯ  ﮐم  اﺳﯽ طﺮﯾﻘﮯ ﺳﮯ  ﭨﺮﯾٹ ،ﮐﺮﯾں  ﺟس طﺮﯾﻘﮯ ﺳﮯ  ﻣوﺟوده  
اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﮐو ﮐﺮﺗﮯ ﮨﯾـــں۔  
 ،ﻣﮩودے ﻣﯾں  دوﺳﺮی ﺑــﺎت ﮐﮩﻧــﺎ ﭼﺎﮨﺘــــﺎ  ،ﮨوں  ﺟو ﺳﻣﺳﯾﺎ اﺑﮫــﯽ  ﺗک ﺑــﻧﯽ  ﮨوﺋــﯽ  ،ﮨﮯ اﺷوﻧﯽ 
وﯾﺷــﻧو  ﺟﯽ ﭘﭼﮫﻠﯽ  ﺑــﺎر ﺳدن ﻣﯾں  ،ﺗﮫﮯ اس وﻗت اﺑﮫــﯽ  ﯾﮩــﺎں  ﻧﮩﯾــں  ،ﮨﯾــں  وه  رﯾﻠــوے ﮐــﺎ  ﻣﺳﺋﻠہ  ﮨﮯ۔ ﮨــم  
اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ،ﮨﯾــں  ﮨﻣـــﺎرا ﭨﯾﻠﯾﻔـــــون  اﭨﮫــﺎﺗﮯ ﮨﯾــں  اور  ﮨﻣـــﺎرا رﯾﺰروﯾﺷن ﮨو  ﺟﺎﺗـــﺎ  ،ﮨﮯ ﻟﯾﮐــن اﯾﮐس 
اﯾم۔ﭘــــﯽ  ﮐو ﺧود ﯾــﺎ ﮐﺳﯽ ﮐو ﮐـــﺎؤﻧﭨﺮ ﭘﺮ  ﺑﮫﯾﺟﻧــــﺎ  ﭘﮍﺗـــﺎ  ،ﮨﮯ اﻧﮐـــﺎ ﭨﯾﻠﯾﻔـــــون  ﺳﮯ  رﯾﺰروﯾﺷن ﭘﺮﻣِﭩِﮉ  
ﻧﮩﯾــں  ﮨﮯ۔ اس ﻣﯾں  ﮐوﺋﯽ ﺑﮩت ﺑــﮍا اﻧوﯾﺳــــﭨﻣﯾﻧٹ ﻧﮩﯾــں  ،ﮨﮯ اﺳﮐﮯ اﻧـــدر  ﮐوﺋﯽ ﺑﮩت ﯾـــﺎدهز ﺧﺮﭼہ ﺑﮫﯽ 
ﻧﮩﯾــں  ﮨﮯ۔ اﮔﺮ  رﯾل ﻣﻧﺘﺮاﻟﯾـــــہ  ﺳﮯ  اﯾــک  ﺳﺮﮐوﻟﺮ ان ﺟﮔﮩوں ﭘﺮ  ﭼﻼ  ،ﺟﺎﺋﯾﮔــــﺎ  ﺟﮩﺎں رﯾﺰروﯾﺷن ﮨوﺗـــﺎ 
ﮨﮯ ﮐہ آپ اﯾﮐس اﯾم۔ﭘـــــﯽ۔  ﮐــﺎ  ﭨﯾﻠﯾﻔـــــون  ﺳﮯ  رﯾﺰروﯾﺷن ﮐﺮ دﯾـــﺎ  ،ﮐﺮﯾں  ﺗو  ﻣﯾﺮے ﺧﯾــﺎل  ﺳﮯ  وه  ﮨو  
ﺟﺎﺋﯾﮔـــــﺎ۔  اس ﻣﯾں  ﮐوﺋﯽ اﯾﮐﺳـــﭨﺮا ﺧﺮﭼہ ﻧہ ﺗو  ﺳﺮﮐﺎر ﭘﺮ  ﭘﮍﻧﮯ واﻻ  ،ﮨﮯ ﻧہ ﮐﺳﯽ ﭘﺮﮐــﺎر ﮐﯽ اﻧــﯽه  
ﮨوﻧﮯ واﻟــﯽ  ﮨﮯ۔ اس ﺳﮯ  اﯾــک  ﺳﻣﺎن ﺑﮫﯽ ﺑﭼــﺎ  رﮨﯾﮔـــﺎ اور  ﺑﮍھﺎﭘﮯ ﻣﯾں  ﻻﺋــن ﻣﯾں  ﻟﮔﻧﮯ ﺳﮯ  ﯾــﺎ ﮐﺳﯽ 
دوﺳﺮے ﮐﯽ ﺧوﺷﺎﻣد ﮐﺮﻧﮯ ﺳﮯ  ﮐہ آپ ﮨﻣـــﺎرا ﮐــﺎرڈ  ﻟﮯ ﺟﺎﮐﺮ وﮨــﺎں رﯾﺰروﯾﺷن ﮐﺮواﮐﺮ  ﻻ  دو — وه  
ﻣﺳﺋﻠہ  ﺑﮫﯽ ﮨل ﮨو  ﺟﺎﺋﯾﮔـــــﺎ۔  ﺟب ﻓون ﺳﮯ  ﺑﮐﻧــگ  ،ﮨوﮔﯽ  ﺗو  ﭨﮐٹ ﮐﯽ ﮐﻧﻔﺮﻣﯾﺷــن  ﮐﮯ ﻟﯾﮯ ﺑﮫﯽ آﺳــﺎﻧﯽ  ﮨو  
،یﺟﺎﺋﯾــگ  ورﻧہ وﮨــﺎں ﺟﺎﮐﺮ ﻟﯾـــﭨﺮ ڈال  ﮐﺮ آﻧـــﺎ ﭘﮍﺗـــﺎ  ﮨﮯ۔ ،ﺳﺮ ﯾہ دوﺳﺮا ﻣﺳﺋﻠہ  ﮨﮯ اور  ﻣﯾﺮے ﺧﯾــﺎل  
ﺳﮯ  اس ﭘﺮ  آپ ،ﮐو  ﻣﻧـــﺘﺮﯾوں اور  ﺳﺮﮐﺎر ﮐو ﺳـــﻧﮔﯾﺎن  ﻟﯾﻧــــﺎ ﭼـــﺎﮨﯾﺋﮯ۔  
 ،ﻣﮩودے ﺗﯾﺳﺮی اور  آﺧﺮی ،ﺑــﺎت ﺟو اور  ﺳـــﺎﺗﮫﯾوں  ﻧﮯ ﺑﮫﯽ ﮐﮩﯽ ،ﮨﮯ وه  ﯾہ ﮨﮯ ﮐہ اس ﻧــﺋﯽ  
ﺑﻠـــڈﻧگ  ﮐﮯ اﻧـــدر  ﮨﻣﯾــں ﺳــﯾﻧﭨﺮل ﮨــﺎل ﮐﯽ ﺑﮩت زﯾـــﺎده ﮐﻣﯽ ﻣﺣﺳوس ﮨوﺗــﯽ  ﮨﮯ۔ 
 ﺳــﯾﻧﭨﺮل ﮨــﺎل ﮐﯽ ﺑﮩت زﯾـــﺎده ﮐﻣﯽ ﻣﺣﺳوس ﮨوﺗــﯽ  ﮨﮯ۔ ﭘﭼﮫﻠﮯ ﮐﭼﮫ دﻧــوں  ﻣﯾں  ﺗو  اﯾﺳــﺎ دور 
ﺑﮫﯽ دﯾﮐﮫـــﺎ ﮐہ ﺳدن ﺳﮯ  ﻧﮐﻠﺘــﮯ  وﻗت ﮨﻣﯾــں اﭘﻧﮯ  اوﭘــﺮ ﺷﺮم آﺗــﯽ  ﺗﮫــﯽ۔ 20-20 ﺳﺎل ﮐﮯ اﯾـــم  ،ﭘﯽ  ﺟو ﮐہ 
اﯾﮐس ﮨو  ﮔﺋﮯ ،ﺗﮫﮯ ﺳﯾﮍھﯾوں ﮐﮯ ﺑـــﺎﮨﺮ  ﮨﯽ ﻣﻧــﮫ  اﭨﮫـــﺎﮐﺮ ﮐﮫﮍے رﮨﺘﮯ ﺗﮫﮯ ﮐہ ﮐوﺋﯽ ،آﺋﯾﮔــــﺎ وت  ﮨــم  
اﺳﮐﮯ ذرﯾﻌﮯ ﺳﻧدﯾش ﺑﮫﺟــــواﺋﯾﻧﮔﮯ۔ ﺣﺎﻻﻧﮐـــہ اب دو ﺗﯾــن  ﺳﯾﺷﻧس ﺳﮯ  ﯾہ ﻣﺳﺋﻠہ  ﺗو  ﮨل ﮨو  ﮔﯾـــﺎ  ،ﮨﮯ 
اﯾﮐس اﯾﻣﭘــــﯾﺰ اﻧـــدر  آﺟﺎﺗﮯ ،ﮨﯾــں  ﻟﯾﮐــن اﻧـــدر  آﻧﮯ ﮐﮯ ﺑﻌــد  وه  ﮐﮩﺎں ﺟــﺎﺋﯾں؟ وه  ﺳﺎﻣﻧﮯ ﮐﮫﮍے ﮨوﺗﮯ

SHRI SAKET GOKHALE (West Bengal) : Mr. Vice Chairman.  Sir, I can never forget 
the day of 11 th July, 2023. I was at that point livin g in Kolkata, having spent about six 
months in jail in a politically-motivated cas e where I was imprisoned in Gujarat on the 
grand charge of money laundering five hundre d rupees. It had been two months, just 
two months, since I had been released from jail -- I was released in May and this was 
July --  on that evening of 11 th July, Sir, I suddenly got a call from my leader,          
Shri Abhishek Banerjee, at 10 p.m., when he told me, ‘Saket, our Party would like to 
see you in the Rajya Sabha. Wish you the best of luck and we expect that you give 
your best.’  Sir, this was not a call that an average person can ever expect to get. For 
a young 36 year old man, with no political  background, who grew up in a 450 square 
feet one bedroom house, I had never imagined  this even in my dreams.  Growing up 
with the middle class, we are taught early that dreams should be the same size as our 
modest houses. You dream as big as your house is.  In the middle class, where we 
come from, we are always told that politic s is not for people like us, which is why I 
want to start by expressing the deepes t gratitude to my l eaders, Ms. Mamata 
Banerjee and Mr. Abhishek Banerjee, who show ed me that there is  indeed a place in 
politics for young people, no matter wher e you come from, as long as you are 
prepared to give your 100 pe r cent in the service of the nation and our people.  I am 
extremely proud to say that I belong to a pa rty where the only criteria that matter are 
your merit and performance, and not yo ur family background. I was extremely 
fortunate to take my oath in the old Par liament building. In every moment I spent 
there, I could feel the spirits of our nati on’s founders reminding me of the immense 
responsibility that I had been entrusted with.  I spent numer ous moments sitting 
[ 18 March, 2026 ]

quietly in the Central Hall and being in awe of the values and struggles that our great 
nation and its Constitution have been founded upon.

Singh.  As a journalist, I had a brief enco unter with him in 2011 in Kuala Lumpur where

to India and serve the nation.  I never thou ght that providence wo uld work in such a

SHRI NARHARI AMIN (Gujarat) : &Hon. Vice-Chairman Sir, on 21 June 2020, during 
the challenging period of the coronavirus pandemic, I was given the opportunity to 
enter this august House as a Member of th e Rajya Sabha.  I would like to express my 
gratitude to the dynamic Prime Minister of our country, Narendra Modi; the Home 
Minister, Amit Shah; the National President of  the Bharatiya Janata Party, Shri J. P. 
Nadda; the former Chief Minister of Gujarat,  Shri Vijay Rupani; the President of the 
Bharatiya Janata Party in Gujarat, Shri Jitu Vaghani; the State Home Minister,        
Shri Pradeepsinh Jadeja; and General Secret ary, Shri Bhikhubhai Dalsania. With their 
support, I had the privilege of being elected to the Rajya Sabha in 2020. As a Member 
of the Rajya Sabha, I had the opportunity to serv e for the past six   ye ars  to   be  a   
member   of  the   Standing   Committee of the Ministry of Railways. During this time, I 
witnessed the transformative decisions take n under the leadership of Prime Minister 
Narendra Modi—from the upgradation of railway stations to efforts aimed at making 
the entire railway system more beneficial to the people. In addition, I served as a 
member of the Consultative Committee of the Ministry of Chemicals and Fertilizers, as 
well as the Consultative Committee of the Finance Ministry. I also had the privilege of 
                   
& English translation of the original speech delivered in Gujarati. 
102
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
working under the guidance of the Chairman of the Rajya Sabha and three           
Vice-Presidents of the country—Shri M. Ve nkaiah Naidu, Shri Jagdeep Dhankhar, 
and    Shri C. P. Radhakrishnan.  I also had the opportunity to wo rk with Shri Piyush 
Goyal, the Leader of the House, and with Sh ri J. P. Nadda. During my tenure in the 
Rajya Sabha, I participated in discussion s on several importan t Bills, including the 
Wakf Bill, the Women’s Reservation Bill, th e VB-G RAM G Act, the Labour Bill, and 
the Tribhuvan Das Cooperative University Bi ll. Over these six years, the Government 
under the hon. Prime Minister Shri Narendrabhai Modi commemorated significant 
milestones, including the 150th birth anni versary of Mahatma Gandhi, as well as 
anniversaries associated with Vande Mata ram, Shri Birsa Munda, Shri Sardar 
Vallabhbhai Patel, and Shri Subhas Chandra Bose. I consider it an honour to have 
been part of this Government during such occasions. 
Sir, I would also like to briefly reflect on  my political journey. As a student of 
New High School, I was elected General Secretary in the 10th st andard among 4,000 
students. Later, at Gujarat Commerce College, I was again elected General 
Secretary, and subsequently served as a Se nate member in the Law Faculty. For 37 
years, I had the opportunity to serve as a Senate member and Syndicate member at 
the Gujarat University, the largest university in the state. In 1980 , I was elected as a 
corporator from the Naranpura area of Ah medabad. In 1990, I was elected as an 
MLA, and the same year, I wa s entrusted with the responsi bility of serving as Home 
Minister. In 1993, at the age of 38, I had the honour of serving as the Deputy Chief 
Minister of Gujarat, handling the Home, Education, and Sports Departments. 
Alongside my political work, I have remained  actively involved in social and religious 
activities. I manage an old-age home, run a school, and operate an Annapurna 
Bhojnalaya, among other initiatives. 
Finally, Sir, I would like to extend a sp ecial note of thanks to the Congress 
Party. During the elections held in the COVID period, five candidates contested—
three from the Bharatiya Janata Party an d two from the Congress. To ensure my 
victory, eight Congress MLAs resigned from their positions as well as from the primary 
membership of their party. I am also pleas ed to say that among these developments, 
I was elected to the Rajya Sabha with the highest number of votes. Furthermore, in 
the eight constituencies where Congress had been elected for many years, the 
subsequent by-elections resulted in victor ies for Bharatiya Janata Party candidates. 
For this, I express my sincere gratitude to the people of Gujarat and thank the voters 
for their support. Thank you very much.
